# Memopai viral playbook — living document

Every run READS this first, then UPDATES it (sections 2–4) after checking the numbers. Keep it short and concrete: rules that change what the next video looks like. Delete rules that the data stops supporting.

## 1. Category rotation (fixed)
**SUPERSEDED 2026-11-26 21:30 (owner): each of the 10 daily slots now has its own SERIES (RUNBOOK 0h): 08 REMINDER OF THE DAY, 10 FUN FACT, 12 STORY, 13 WHAT IS MEMOPAI, 14 HALF-HALF, 16 POLICEMAN, 18 FUN FACT 2, 19 LIFE WITH vs WITHOUT, 20 STORY 2, 22 REMINDER FOR TOMORROW. Log the series as the category. The rotation below is history.**
Rotate strictly, based on the last entry in `topics_log.md`: **STORY → FUN FACT → PRODUCTIVITY → STORY → …**
Since 2026-11-11 evening EVERY category must pass the Memopai fit test (RUNBOOK section 0c): the story is about forgetting or reminding, it falls apart without Memopai, and it shows only real features.
- **STORY**: a character comedy or mini-drama where forgetting (or having to nag someone) is the problem and one WhatsApp message to Memopai is the turning point. Gary is allowed only when the plot is about remembering. No more "arrest <random thing> + Memopai cameo".
- **FUN FACT**: a true, verified fact about memory, forgetting, time or habits, or a real "someone forgot one thing and it cost a fortune" moment, followed by "same situation, with one reminder".
- **PRODUCTIVITY**: an everyday pain everyone knows (free trial renews, bins day, birthdays, parking, kids' pickup, bills, follow-ups, nagging a flatmate), solved on screen by texting Memopai.

## 3. What the data says so far (hypotheses, keep testing)
- 2026-11-27 21:00 (22:00 run): series ranking after day 1 of v4: STORY (12) 1,619 / 14.4 s and POLICEMAN low-poly 3D (16) 1,544 / 17.75 s are the only hits; kinetic type, documentary, explainer card, split screen and paper cut-out all sat at 130-173 with 5.4-9.6 s. What the two hits share: CHARACTERS with faces doing something absurd/relatable in a real set + a quoted or shouted line, and a deadpan escalating plot; what the misses share: no character the viewer identifies with in the first 2 s (animal, type, card, paper). This run retests the Mom recipe on Grandma with the funniest universal artefact we have not used: Grandma's WhatsApp video call with her face far too close to the camera, greeting callback 'Hello, stranger!' → 'Hello, favourite!', weekly recurring reminder, and a hard cut (story ≤22 s).

- 2026-11-11 19:25: 'Time' (busy, 13 shots, freeze gimmick) = 247 views / 34% retention after 2 h; Snowman (one clear idea) climbed to 1,911. Confirms owner feedback: busy = dead. TikTok blocked for 24 h after the 18:00 API error, so skip TikTok until ~2026-11-12 18:00 Zagreb.
- 2026-11-11 18:15 OWNER FEEDBACK (top priority): stories are bad: too much happening, hard to keep up, they don't make sense. RUNBOOK section 2 was rewritten (Story rules v2: one idea, three beats, max 8 lines/8 shots, one text element at a time, clarity gate with a fresh subagent). The numbers agree: retention % (avg watch ÷ length) is best for single-idea videos (Crow 61%, Moon 54%, Karma 54%, Snowman 53%) and worst for the busy multi-idea ones (Door 40%, Brain 40%, Wombat quiz 36%). The 18:00 'Time' video (13 shots, freeze + text + play + birthday + release) is an example of what NOT to do.
- TikTok rejects API posts after ~15 in 24 h ('large number of posts published through the API'); the 18:00 TikTok post failed. Do not post every slot to TikTok until the owner decides the cadence.
- 17:22 UPDATE: Buffer reels are NOT suppressed on IG; metrics just lag 12–20 h. Ignore 'null' early numbers.
- QUESTION/QUIZ covers flop (wombat quiz 300, brain-3AM 198) vs 'This cop arrested/hated…' CLAIM covers (1.6k–2.5k). Use claim covers; drop quiz/2nd-person-question openers.
- Best two formats: (a) real animal/history fact wrapped in a Gary story (crow 2,463, goldfish 2,148); (b) Gary arrests ONE abstract/iconic thing that gets revenge (Karma 2,209 with 25.5 s avg watch = best retention; gravity, shadow). Revenge/comeback twist by the arrested thing keeps people watching.
- PRODUCTIVITY so far underperforms (door 686, brain 198); keep it, but wrap it in the 'This cop…' claim hook.
- "He/This cop arrested <impossible thing>" hooks win on Instagram (1.8k–2.2k) and beat plain history facts (200–600).
- A fact wrapped in a character story (goldfish, 1,171) beats the same kind of fact told as a history lesson.
- Covers with a bold absurd claim + character face beat "TRUE STORY" date-label covers.
- Average watch time is ~14 s on 30 s videos: the middle sags. Front-load, cut setup, put a second hook at ~8–10 s, and try 20–25 s lengths.
- Posting the same story twice (Napoleon) split views; never repeat.
- Winners (1.8k–2.2k) were all posted ~05:00 UTC (06:00 Zagreb) in one batch; same-format posts in other batches got 200–300. Posting hour/batch may matter as much as the hook. The new hourly slots will tell us.
- The two "single impossible arrest" hooks with a physical/natural target (gravity+sun, shadow) beat lists of holiday characters (Santa/Easter Bunny 264, Santa/GPS/sock 306). Prefer ONE iconic natural target over a list of three.
- Best avg watch among stories is 19.8 s (Autocorrect, multi-case) vs ~14 s typical: more mini-payoffs = better retention.
- TikTok: the plain product reels (700–950 views) beat every cartoon (0–120). Cartoon reach on TikTok collapsed right when batch-posting 5+ videos within minutes started (Nov 9 18:42) — spacing posts hourly may be what fixes it; watch the hourly slots.
- Plain 'Memopai.com' product reels keep pulling 1.5k–2.8k IG views even today (08:48 reel 1,565 in a few hours); the product itself, shown simply, is a proven IG hook. Consider making the Memopai moment in cartoons more visible and earlier (not an ad, but a clear 'oh I want that' beat).
- TikTok gets nothing: test TikTok-native elements (on-screen question in first frame, faster first cut, trending-topic hooks, 3–5 hashtags not 6) and note whether anything moves.

## 5. Standing rules for studying viral content
- 2026-11-21 11:00 research note: 'Mom never answers her phone / her phone is always dead' is a recurring adult-child viral theme (Motherly 'This TikTok shows exactly why moms never return phone calls', Distractify 'man revealed his true feelings about his mom not answering her phone', Scary Mommy/Motherly 'how often do you call your mom' TikTok). Mechanic borrowed: the unanswered call + her cheerful 'phone died!' callback; WhatsApp's single grey tick (sent, not delivered) as the proof. Script, visuals and jokes original.
- 2026-11-17 17:05 research note: 'Mom wants her Tupperware back' is a recurring cross-country family meme (N+ Mexico: Argentine mom cries on TikTok when her daughter returns all her containers; El Imparcial: same genre; Briefly/IOL South Africa: mom packs the kids' food in shopping bags to protect her Tupperware; Yahoo/AOL viral Pyrex feud 'you don't send glass Tupperware with guests'). Fact verified on 2+ sources: about 43% of daily behaviour is habit performed in the same context while thinking about something else (Wood, Quinn & Kashy 2002, JPSP diary study; APA Monitor 2020; Lumen Learning intro psych). Mechanic borrowed: Mom's question at the door + the stack in your cupboard; script, visuals and jokes original.
- 2026-11-17 11:00 research note: unused/forgotten gift cards are a steady money-news + relatable topic (Bankrate gift-card surveys: 43–47% of US adults hold at least one unused card, avg ~$175–187; Fox Business '$21B in unused gift cards'; Daily Dot viral gift-card stories such as the 20-year-old Walmart card). Mechanic borrowed: the printed expiry date as the proof + the giver asking how it was.
- 2026-11-16 21:00 research note: the screenshot folder as a self-roast is a recurring relatable theme (AOL/Independent 'forget the Notes app, the screenshot folder is the most exposing part of your phone', Know Your Meme 'explaining my camera roll', VegOut 'people who screenshot everything just in case', several 'no storage: the endless camera roll' essays). Fact verified: Sparrow, Liu & Wegner 2011 (Science, 'Google effects on memory'): people who expected information to be saved remembered it less (Columbia News; Scientific American). Mechanic borrowed: the camera-roll count as frame-1 proof + self-roast; script, visuals and jokes are original.
- 2026-11-16 13:00 research note: 'who's booking?' / the friend who says 'I'll book it' and doesn't is a recurring friend-group joke (Know Your Meme 'the group chat' family, group-chat TikTok skits, restaurant-meme round-ups such as Toast's 'relatable restaurant memes'); mechanic borrowed: the host's polite 'I don't see anything under that name' + everyone turning to look at the one who said they'd book it.
- 2026-11-16 11:05 research note: last-minute kids'-party gift panic is a recurring parent topic (Motherly 'birthday present hack so you never panic over a last-minute gift', SheKnows kids gift hacks, Motherly 'two party invites, almost no budget'); mechanic borrowed: the kid ready at the door asking for the present + the improvised wrap of whatever is in the cupboard.
- 2026-11-16 08:00 research note: 'I'll text you good luck' / forgetting a partner's big day (interview, exam, first day) is a recurring relationship-confession theme; the stronger mechanic is the viewer's OWN typed promise shown back to them (same family as the 'sorry, just saw this' and 'let's grab coffee soon' artefacts that drove our best comments). Fact verified on 2 sources: prospective memory (remembering to do something later) lapses are the most common everyday memory failures (Niedźwieńska et al. 2020, PLoS ONE 15(9):e0239581: 64% of 1,319 diary-recorded failures; Dismukes 2012, Current Directions in Psychological Science / APS release 'When we forget to remember').
- 2026-11-15 19:05 research note: 'text me when you get home' is a widely shared friendship ritual (Distractify on the posts that went viral after Sarah Everard's death; Daily Dot/Imgflip texting memes; Motherly's 'texting back takes 3–5 business days' reel) and forgetting to send it is a classic guilt moment. Handle with care: the friend's worry is real, keep it light ('I almost called your mum!'), never trivialise safety. Mechanic borrowed: the 01:12 lock screen with missed calls + the friend's relieved line.
- 2026-11-15 17:05 research note: 'not me' relatable moments keep driving engagement (thesocialcat 'Not me' / 'Why is this me' glossaries, Know Your Meme relatable-memes collections); 'are you even listening?' / mental-load partner moments are a staple. Fact verified: Masicampo & Baumeister 2011, 'Consider it done! Plan making can eliminate the cognitive effects of unfulfilled goals' (JPSP 101(4), PubMed 21688924; Psychology Today and Wake Forest coverage). Mechanic borrowed: the partner's 'you didn't hear a word' + the looping thought made visible.
- 2026-11-15 15:10 research note: 'default parent' / mental-load reels keep going viral (ParentMap on default-parent Instagram reels, Motherly's mental-load and soap-dispenser pieces), and the forgotten PE kit is an evergreen school-run pain (NationalWorld 'last-minute PE kit hacks', Mumsnet AIBU 'not sending my child's PE kit'). Mechanic borrowed: the kid's 'can you bring it?' call mid-morning + the parent as the family's reminder.

- 2026-11-13 19:00 research note: 2026 relatable-format round-ups (thesocialcat 'Not me' moments, socialbee trending IG memes, the 'Netflix documentary' narrated-mundane-fail meme) keep rewarding a tiny, oddly specific everyday failure narrated with mock seriousness; mechanic borrowed: the narrator calmly naming the exact object/date that proves the fail ('Return by the twelfth. Mechanic borrowed: the exact promise people say, counted ('four times since March').
- 2026-11-14 17:05 research note: last-minute / belated birthday memes and 'forgot mom's birthday' confessions stay an evergreen relatable genre (Scary Mommy, Bustle and Motherly meme round-ups, 'last-minute birthday memes' pieces); the family group chat where a sibling posts first is the everyday artefact. Mechanic borrowed: the exact polite guilt line ('Oh… you remembered.') + the sibling who always wins.
- 2026-11-15 11:05 research note: lending money to friends and having to awkwardly ask for it back is an evergreen relatable money topic (Inquirer 'Friends, loans and consequences', MIT Sloan 'lenders have blind trust, borrowers have blind spots', 'the Venmo-later problem' explainers); research: Loewenstein & Dezsö 2012 (J. Econ. Psych., Carnegie Mellon, 971 people) borrowers more often believe a loan was repaid as agreed while lenders of overdue loans expect never to see it; Morvinski & Shani 2022 (OBHDP) misaligned borrower/lender mindsets on small loans. Mechanic borrowed: the lender's polite 'No rush!' as the cringe line.
- 2026-11-15 13:05 research note: expired-passport-at-the-airport stories are a recurring news/viral genre (Examiner Live: Ryanair refused a husband over his passport; inkl: man at the airport for a snowboarding trip notices his passport date; KHOU/10TV 'verify' pieces on the 6-month validity rule; Fox 'denied boarding' travel-expert round-ups). - 2026-11-15 21:00 research note: the forgotten tooth fairy is an evergreen parent-confession genre (6abc 'fed-up 9-year-old scolds forgetful tooth fairy in viral letter', GMA on Idina Menzel's 'bad moms' tooth-fairy moment, Tinybeans '18 reasons the tooth fairy was a no-show' / excuse lists, Motherwell 'how to save face when the tooth fairy forgets'). Mechanic borrowed: the kid's betrayed morning line + the parent's improvised excuse.
- 2026-11-18 15:05 research note: the planning fallacy is well documented (Buehler, Griffin & Ross 1994: students' thesis estimates ~34 days vs ~56 actual; Kahneman & Tversky 1979 coined it; Wikipedia, fourweekmba, siliconcanals 2026 explainer); mechanic borrowed: the 'next week' self-promise + the late-delivery printout as the cost image; script, visuals and jokes original.
- 2026-11-19 19:05 research note: the teen brain is still maturing into the mid-to-late twenties, with the prefrontal cortex (planning, prioritising) among the last areas to mature (NIMH 'The Teen Brain: 7 Things to Know'; URMC Health Encyclopedia 'Understanding the Teen Brain': rational part not fully developed until ~25). Locked-out / forgotten-keys moments are a recurring family-viral genre (We Got This Covered siblings-keys-on-roof clip, Upworthy/AOL 'toddler unlocks door for locked-out mom', Says.com man drives 18 h and forgot his keys). Mechanic borrowed: the one-line text from the doorstep + the keys visible inside; script, visuals and jokes original.
- 2026-11-20 17:05 research note: 'why does the smoke alarm always chirp at 3 AM' is an evergreen relatable complaint (Kidde support article 'Why do smoke alarms always chirp in the middle of the night?', First Alert 'Safety Corner' blog, Farmers' Almanac): low batteries chirp in the small hours because the house is coolest then and the battery's internal resistance rises. Mechanic borrowed: a question everyone has asked out loud, held open and answered after the product beat.
- 2026-11-23 08:10 research note: last-minute school requests are a live parent-humour genre (Upworthy 'comedian nails the obscure last-minute school requests kids drop on parents at 10pm'; Daily Dot 'mom collapses on stairs after realising she has 14 more years of school mornings'; Upworthy 'mom's rant about why she was late to drop-off'). Mechanic borrowed: the school-morning panic over one forgotten school item. Format-reach note: 2026 write-ups compare reel vs carousel reach by account size (Fanpage Karma 'which format delivers the most reach at each account size', Upgrow 'organic reach by account size & format'); our own numbers (carousels reach 1-2) are the deciding data.
- Each run, spend a few minutes on web search for what is going viral NOW: top AI-animated shorts, viral story formats, trending topics, hook formulas. Write down any new pattern in section 3.
- Borrow the *mechanics* (hook structure, pacing, twist shape, cover style, topic angle), then write an original script, original visuals and original jokes. Never copy another creator's script, characters, footage, audio or a copyrighted character; never use real people's likenesses.
- Every claim presented as fact must be verified on 2+ sources.
